#2786. Steelers defense scores two touchdowns in dominant win over Broncos

After a tumultuous week of horrible PR featuring Aaron Rodgers being released from the team, the Pittsburgh Steelers bounced back after two consecutive losses in the biggest way possible, scoring two defensive touchdowns en route to a 27-5 win over the Denver Broncos.

The Steelers' defense set the tone early on. After the offense punted following an eight-play drive, Bo Nix dropped the ball after a pump fake, which rolled into the end zone and was picked up by T.J. Watt for a touchdown to put the Steelers up 7-0. Denver then went three-and-out, and a Mason Rudolph hit Germie Bernard on a short crosser that the rookie took for a 58-yard catch-and-run. Three plays later, Rudolph connected with DK Metcalf for a touchdown on a one-on-one fade route over Pat Surtain. The 14-0 lead also marked the first time since 2019 that Pittsburgh scored 14 points in the first quarter.

Pittsburgh's defense wasn't done scoring, though. After a safety and a field goal made the game 14-5, Chris Boswell kicked a field goal with less than two minutes to play in the half, extending the lead to 17-5. On the ensuing drive, Nix threw a pass that looked as if it was intended for Jalen Ramsey, who took the interception 36 yards to the end zone to give the Steelers a 24-5 lead at the half. A Chris Boswell field goal in the fourth quarter would be the final points of the game, as Pittsburgh wins 27-5 and climbs to the No. 6 seed in the AFC.

The Steelers sacked Bo Nix three times and held him to just 169 yards passing on an astoundingly low 4.2 yards per attempt. Denver couldn't run the ball, either, totaling just 73 yards on 26 carries. Overall, Denver had just 205 yards on three yards per play.

Mason Rudolph didn't throw the ball a ton, but he made his throws count. He finished the day 9-of-15 for 142 yards and a touchdown. He had completions of 58, 37, and 22 yards, pushing the ball downfield and proving to be a steady hand to complement the run game.

The Steelers ran the ball often, totaling 117 yards on 31 carries. It wasn't an explosive run game, but it was effective to help Pittsburgh get a much-needed win.

Pittsburgh now prepares for a massive Week 13 Sunday night game against the Houston Texans, who are fighting for their playoff lives at 4-6.

#2784. What we learned as Warriors' valiant comeback attempt falls short vs. Blazers

What we learned as Warriors' valiant comeback attempt falls short vs. Blazers originally appeared on NBC Sports Bay Area

During Steve Kerr's pregame news conference before his Warriors faced the Trail Blazers on Friday night in Portland, he raised a caution flag big enough to cover Oregon.

"We're finding our rhythm offensively since the (road) trip where we really struggled," Kerr told reporters at Moda Center. "But this team is a different animal defensively. They're going to test us way more than the last couple games felt. We're going to feel that pressure. We're going to see how we can respond to it."

Not well at all, as the Warriors' one-game road trip ended with a 116-94 loss that eliminated them from NBA Cup competition.

Four Warriors scored in double figures, led by Yaxel Lendeborg's 18 points. De'Anthony Melton had 17, Brandin Podziemski 16 and Charles Bassey 12.

Portland, playing without Damian Lilliard and Ja Morant, limited the Warriors to 36.4-percent shooting from the field, including 33.3 percent from distance.

Here are three observations from the loss that dropped Golden State's record to 10-9:

The same Warriors who have won eight consecutive games at home have lost six straight as visitors because they are not the same Warriors.

The "home" Warriors apply ball movement and accurate shooting, and they turn up their defense when needed. The "visitor" Warriors enter an arena and immediately fall apart on both ends.

Golden State fell behind so quickly and decisively that its 14th point didn't arrive until the game's 15th minute. The Blazers by then had piled up 47 points. The Warriors were singing the same song as on their last road trip, when they lost the last four games by an average of 27.3 points.

The Warriors once again didn't match the energy of the home team, seemingly taken aback by Portland's intensity on both ends. They fell behind by 24 within nine minutes before finding enough offense in the second quarter to trim a 36-point deficit to 23 (67-44) at the half.

The first step to curing this ailment is to start taking their Chase Center vitality on the road.

While the first quarter was reminiscent of Golden State's first quarters on its last road trip, there was a striking difference that might be significant in upcoming games.

This time, the Warriors would not allow themselves to be humiliated into submission.

It was as if looking at the scoreboard early in the second quarter and seeing a 36-point deficit pumped wolverine blood through the Warriors' collective bodies.

Putting some screws into their loose defense and regaining familiarity with the hoop, Golden State won the second quarter by three (33-30) behind an offense buoyed by Melton's three triples.

The Warriors came out after halftime and rallied behind Lendeborg's 12 points in 10 minutes to win the third quarter by 10 (34-24) and enter the fourth trailing 91-78 – close enough for a fighting chance.

Ultimately, with the Blazers regaining their first-quarter zest, it was not enough. For that, the Warriors can blame their first 14 minutes.

Bassey made his fifth consecutive start at center, and there is no reason to believe he will be heading back to the bench anytime soon.

First off, Bassey at center allows Draymond Green the freedom to stray from the paint, defend perimeter players and play the passing lanes. That versatility tends to help the team's overall defense.

Secondly, Bassey's soft shooting touch makes him a potent scoring threat in in the paint. He has scored in double figures in all five starts, averaging 12.2 points per game.

His 12 points on this night came on 6-of-9 shooting from the field. He added five rebounds and is averaging 8.4 per game since moving into the starting lineup.

Bassey continues to work on his screening, which often isn't as bad as it looks. His illegal-screen turnovers should decrease with better timing with his ball-handling teammates.

#2781. Lazio, compleanno speciale per Maurizio Manzini: «È orgoglio vedere la squadra lì in classifica»

Le dichiarazioni dell'ex team manager della Lazio sul cammino in campionato

Compleanno dolce per Maurizio Manzini, storico team manager della Lazio, intervenuto a Radiosei durante ‘NMM‘  per commentare il momento positivo dei biancocelesti.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Questo mio compleanno lo festeggio in un momento piacevole dal punto di vista sportivo. È orgoglio vedere la Lazio lì in classifica, grazie al lavoro di Rino e della squadra. È un grande merito loro ed una grande soddisfazione per noi. Come andrà a finire la stagione? Non lo dico per scaramanzia, diciamo che speriamo di salvarci.

Il legame tra Maurizio Manzini e il club capitolino nasce da lontano, già nel 1971, quando da dirigente nel settore dell'aviazione iniziò a supportare volontariamente la società nella gestione logistica e delle trasferte, conquistando la stima di figure leggendarie come Tommaso Maestrelli. Dopo un primo rifiuto, nella stagione 1988/89 accettò ufficialmente l'incarico di team manager, diventando per decenni l'anello di congiunzione indispensabile tra spogliatoio e vertici societari. Poliglotta, apprezzato da arbitri, colleghi e persino dagli avversari per stile e riservatezza, ha rappresentato la memoria storica del club fino al graduale passaggio di consegne avvenuto a partire dall'annata 2014/15.

#2779. T.J. Watt, Steelers defense make statement in first game without Aaron Rodgers

PITTSBURGH – The Steelers punctuated a wild week that included the furious departure of future Hall of Fame quarterback Aaron Rodgers with an exclamation point on Black Friday.

Embattled coach Mike McCarthy's team secured an impressive 27-5 win against the Denver Broncos on Nov. 27 at Acrisure Stadium.

The defense generated a pair of touchdowns while Mason Rudolph, Rodgers' backup the past couple of seasons, led the offense to enough points to cruise amid struggles from the Broncos' own signal caller, Bo Nix.

Here's USA TODAY's report card on the action from a Pittsburgh perspective.

Required reading: Steelers fans spare no one amid Aaron Rodgers, Mike McCarthy feud

McCarthy didn't ask Rudolph to do too much within the game plan, and he didn't really have to as the defense led the scoring. Rudolph completed nine of 15 passes for 142 yards and a touchdown. Most of the throws were on the shorter and safer side, though he did seem more comfortable and willing to stand in the pocket to look downfield than Rodgers has been the past couple of weeks. If that continues, it will be a positive development for this offense. At the same time, it's way too soon to declare whether he'll be a better long-term option than Rodgers.

It was a third tepid week in a row for Jaylen Warren and Rico Dowdle, despite McCarthy leaning heavily into the running game by close to a 2-to-1 margin. Dowdle's 27-yard run in the third quarter helped goose the stats to over 100 yards for the day, but he gave the momentum right back by fumbling on the next play. Remove that big gainer from the equation and the rushing average was well below four yards per touch for the duo. Blocking is undeniably a problem, but neither guy is forcing many misses to bail the blockers out either. They've got to be better.

Rookie Germie Bernard generated the explosive play this offense has sorely lacked with a 58-yard catch and run in the first quarter. DK Metcalf later cashed that possession in with a 22-yard touchdown catch from Rudolph that represented his first meaningful first-half production in several weeks. It was an encouraging step forward after last week's multiple altercations with the Philadelphia Eagles that were largely forgotten once the drama with Rodgers began to unfold. Michael Pittman Jr. was rather quiet in his return from injury, but his presence seemed to help get Metcalf into single coverage. Roman Wilson added a 37-yard catch in the fourth quarter that helped salt the victory away.

Pittman's return and the heavy dose of runs marginalized Pat Freiermuth and Darnell Washington in the passing game. Both were among Rodgers' favorite targets, but it remains to be seen how much Rudolph does or does not want to utilize them, especially with his receiving group now mostly healthy for the first time since early in the season.

The protection of Rudolph was pretty good. He was sacked just once and had the luxury of several extended drops. His mobility relative to Rodgers' no doubt helped these guys look better. The run blocking still simply has not found its groove in the three games that left guard Mason McCormick has largely missed. Position coach James Campen has his work cut out for him in getting this group ready for December and January football.

Denver managed just 22 yards on the ground in the first half as the Pittsburgh front flew to the ball and staked itself to a comfortable lead. There were several tackles for a loss mixed in, and that forced Nix to throw more often than Denver probably wanted him to. Tough rush defense was a hallmark of Pittsburgh's four-game winning streak earlier in the season. If the guys up front can maintain this form, it will help as the weather conditions deteriorate down the stretch.

T.J. Watt recovered a fumble in the end zone for a touchdown in the first quarter to loudly announce his presence after a couple of quiet weeks in which he generated just one pressure in the two losses to Cincinnati and Baltimore. He added a fourth-quarter sack. Nick Herbig returned from injury with a key second-quarter sack that pushed the Broncos out of field goal range and thwarted a scoring threat. Payton Wilson and Patrick Queen were active again, each notching tackles for loss while helping the defensive front lock down on the Denver running game.

Cornerback/safety Jalen Ramsey intercepted Nix and returned it 36 yards for a touchdown shortly before halftime. It was one of the biggest plays he's made in Pittsburgh since general manager Omar Khan traded for him before the 2025 season. Asante Samuel Jr. added a fourth-quarter pick that solidified a comfortable lead. Nix generally struggled to make things happen against the Steelers' soft zone coverage scheme. Ramsey and Co. mostly kept the play in front of them and forced Denver to take small chunks at a time without making mistakes. Nix failed in that regard, so it was a nice day for this group that has really only struggled against Cincinnati's Joe Burrow over the past couple of months.

Patrick Graham's defense came ready to play at home. The disruptiveness and splash plays were badly needed and gave this offense room to breathe in front of a home crowd that probably would not have been friendly had Pittsburgh fallen behind. It was hard to see the "spark" McCarthy sought from the offense, though. The passing game still lacks verticality and McCarthy seems to want to force the run when it's not there in the absence of Rodgers to push back on him. For that reason, he's got to own it more than ever when the plan doesn't work particularly well. The offense did score just 13 points, after all.

This article originally appeared on USA TODAY: T.J. Watt, Steelers defense make statement in first game without Aaron Rodgers

#2778. Horizon rushes by Notre Dame Prep to reach 5A final

Just when it seemed like the game was slipping away from Horizon, sophomore running back Dylan Brush came up big — just like he has time and time again this season for the Huskies.

Brush was the sparkplug in Scottsdale Horizon's 28-21 comeback win over Scottsdale Notre Dame Prep in the 5A semifinals on Nov. 27, scoring two touchdowns to help lead the team. Horizon (10-2) advances to the state championship game at Mountain America Stadium on Dec. 4 against Scottsdale Desert Mountain.

After trailing 14-0, the Huskies clawed their way back to come out on top behind a ferocious rushing attack.

"They're resilient," Horizon coach Tyson Ditmore said. "We've preached that the second half and third quarters are going to be really big for us this year. We came back out, relied on a run game. The kids, they were a little bit more cool, calm and collected. Really proud of the way that they bounced back."

Before Horizon could blink, it was already down 7-0. Notre Dame senior Matt Higby returned the opening kickoff 80 yards to put the Saints up just 12 seconds into the game.

After a quick three-and-out from the Horizon offense on its first drive of the game, Notre Dame responded with a 13-play, 79-yard scoring drive powered by the legs of running back Decker Haskins-Smith. Haskins-Smith, who had 12 runs on the drive, capped it off with a two-yard run to go up 14-0 with 3:23 left in the first.

Horizon's next drive ended up in catastrophe as a fake punt attempt was blown up by Notre Dame lineman James Mariotti, giving the Saints the ball at the Huskies' 36-yard line. But the defense held and forced a field goal attempt, which was blocked by Ryan Rottkamp and returned to Notre Dame's 32-yard line by Garrett Kerr, the first of a few big plays from Kerr on the night.

Just as the Horizon offense was getting into a groove, a tipped pass from quarterback Kai Knudson landed in the hands of defensive back Mason Pavone for an interception. But again the defense held firm and forced a punt to get the ball back.

That's when Brush began to put his stamp on the game. First, it was a 20-yard run to make it 14-7 with 2:33 left in the second quarter. The score provided much-needed life for a stagnating offense.

"We were leaning on him," Ditmore said. "We knew that the runs were going to open for him, and he did an awesome job of just continuing to get downhill. He got us that touchdown to put us right back."

After Brush got the offense going again on the first drive of the second half, he limped off the field after a hit on the sideline. Garrett Kerr entered and immediately popped off two big runs of 20 and 14 yards to get down to the one. Following a penalty from Horizon, Knudson punched it in from six yards on an option run to make it 14-13 following a missed point-after attempt with 7:04 left in the third.

But Notre Dame responded with another 13-play scoring drive, traveling 82 yards in seven minutes to bring the lead to 21-13 with 53 seconds remaining in the third. Haskins-Smith, the only source of offense the whole game for Notre Dame, scored on a two-yard run.

Brush came back on the field on Horizon's next drive and got the Huskies into the red zone before finishing it off with a two-yard touchdown run. Knudson connected with wide receiver James Klopfenstein — who returned after an eight-week absence — for the two-point conversion to tie it 21-21 with 7:57 left in the game.

Then came Notre Dame's only mistake of the game, and it was a costly one. A fumble from Notre Dame was recovered by defensive back Sam Klopfenstein, giving Horizon the ball close to midfield.

On back-to-back drives, it was the Klopfenstein brothers making a difference. The family started a legacy at the school with their older brother, Matthew, who is now at Baylor and played on the team that went to the 2021 title game.

"A family that's been at Horizon for God knows when," Ditmore said. "We wish that they had some more coming through, but Sam is the youngest. James went out and he finished his home game here on his own. Really proud of him. He's going to be back for us, ready to go again."

Two more big runs from Brush pushed the ball into Notre Dame territory. Facing a fourth-and-one and with the game on the line, Ditmore and his staff changed it up — going with Kerr for the play-call instead of Brush. Kerr rushed up the middle gap before bouncing on the outside for a 35-yard touchdown to take a 28-21 lead with 3:01 left in the game.

"He's a senior, it's his dream," Brush said. "We were so close last year to go to state; we lost by a field goal. This time it was amazing. It was right up A gap. He bounces outside, straight line, scores it. We knew we were gonna win. Well, we still (had) a little bit left, but it helped our hopes. I love Garrett Kerr. He puts in so much work throughout the whole year."

A sack from defensive lineman Vedder Wolfely backed up Notre Dame on its next drive. The Saints nearly completed a fourth-and-25 conversion attempt, but the pass was ruled incomplete. Horizon took a knee to end the game, setting up a rematch with the only team other than Notre Dame to beat them this season.

"I would love to have Desert Mountain because they ended our season last year," Brush said. "We want to end their season. We're gonna be ready for them."

It was a bitter end for Notre Dame (9-3), which surprised many by making it this far.

"No one really thought we'd be here," Notre Dame coach George Prelock said. "I think a lot in the preseason thought we'd go 3-7 at best. These boys grinded. We proved a lot people wrong. We really played as a team and came so close. I'm so proud of these boys."

This article originally appeared on Arizona Republic: Horizon rushes by Notre Dame Prep to reach 5A final

#2769. Aaron Rodgers-less Steelers end chaotic week with resounding win over Broncos

Mike McCarthy has smiled before on the football field, in the aftermath of a win, in the walk from the sideline to the locker room as the handshakes unfold. But this one, this grin that turned into a beam by the time he left the grass at Acrisure Stadium after the Steelers' 27-5 win over the Broncos on Friday, felt different.

This one, after a week when Pittsburgh staunchly supported McCarthy when Aaron Rodgers — who led him to the Super Bowl in Green Bay, vouched for him to land the Steelers job and then shredded him in an interview that thrust the NFL's model of stability into a state of turmoil — contained a hint of validation. Less than 24 hours after ex-Steelers head coach Mike Tomlin called their present a "dumpster fire," McCarthy and Mason Rudolph, the two members in the spotlight the most after Rodgers' scathing rant Wednesday, proved they might survive the chaos after all.

With Rodgers now released, with signs and pictures of No. 8 removed from around the stadium, with Pittsburgh just over 48 hours removed from what Prime Video broadcaster Al Michaels described as the "shot heard 'round the football world," the Steelers delivered a resounding statement to move to 7-4. Rudolph completed 9-of-15 passes for 142 yards and a touchdown, converting when needed and facilitating a fast start for Pittsburgh. T.J. Watt and Jalen Ramsey returned a fumble and an interception for a touchdown, respectively, and the Steelers defense silenced Bo Nix (22 of 40, 169 yards, two interceptions).

Pittsburgh Steelers quarterback Mason Rudolph (2) celebrates with Max Iheanachor (71) during an NFL football game in Pittsburgh, Friday, Nov. 27, 2026. AP Photo/David Dermer

"There's a lot going on," McCarthy told reporters. "Any time you're going through a situation where time is taken away from winning and your team responds, it is extremely important and gratifying. You have to make sure you keep it about the players."

Maybe it was the initial roar from the crowd, the one that followed Rudolph's introduction as the starting quarterback, that suggested the Steelers — and, by extension, McCarthy — might survive. Maybe it was the first touchdown pass from Rudolph to DK Metcalf, who'd been ejected from the game last weekend in what ultimately served as one of the final turning points of the Rodgers era in Pittsburgh.

On Monday, after the Steelers dropped a second consecutive game, McCarthy reiterated that Rodgers was his quarterback. One day later, Rudolph was named the Steelers starter and Rodgers was listed as inactive in an 11:10 p.m. X post. Then, on Wednesday, Rodgers went on "The Pat McAfee Show" and claimed that McCarthy fell asleep during meetings, broke down a halftime huddle with "1-2-3 Packers" and missed walk-throughs. Rodgers was released by the Steelers later that day, and the organization has since reiterated its belief in McCarthy.

Rudolph made his 20th career start Friday, but it marked his first since Week 12 of last season. The circumstances surrounding it were shocking too. With their win Friday, the Steelers demonstrated that the trust — in Rudolph by turning to him over young quarterbacks, in McCarthy by keeping him on staff even after the accusations — was warranted. They jumped out to a quick 14-0 lead behind the touchdowns from Watt and Metcalf, and even after the Broncos got on the board with a safety and crept closer with a field goal, Pittsburgh managed to create some breathing room before halftime. Chris Boswell connected on a field goal, and Ramsey returned an errant pass from Nix 36 yards for a touchdown to make it 24-5 with 1:33 remaining in the second quarter.

Pittsburgh Steelers head coach Mike McCarthy walks across the field after an NFL football game against the Denver Broncos in Pittsburgh, Friday, Nov. 27, 2026. AP Photo/Gene J. Puskar

"In a weird way," Rudolph said on the Prime Video broadcast postgame, "the adversity probably brought us closer together."

Neither offense managed much in the second half. Nix struggled the whole game. And Rudolph didn't need to play hero. By that point, the Steelers had already made their statement. By that point, they'd already produced a sample that showed that perhaps the organization isn't scrambling after all, that maybe they can rebound from the chaos Rodgers left them with.

They're now 7-4. They'll be tied with the Bengals atop the AFC North if Cincinnati falls Sunday. And they ended a week of questions with a resounding answer.

"That dude has had to step into some critical situations over his career," defensive tackle Cam Heyward told reporters of Rudolph, "and he's ready for those moments. … He understood that, with everything that went on, he couldn't have a drop-off. And I'm just appreciative to have a guy that s–t hits the fan, he's able to just say, 'All right, I've gotta step up.' "

#2767. ‘He didn’t seem’: Nick Nurse says he has noticed a change in LeBron James’ game after 76ers beat Pacers

The Philadelphia 76ers beat the Indiana Pacers 111-87 on the road, and LeBron James was the star of the show.

The Sixers were in control throughout the game and dominated the hosts. They completely deserved to win and go through to the next round of the NBA Cup.

#2765. Atlanta Hawks (11-6) at Boston Celtics (12-6) Game #19 11/27/26

Atlanta Hawks (11-6) at Boston Celtics (12-6)Friday, November 27, 20268:00 PM ETRegular Season Game #19, Home Game #8TV: NBCSB, ANF/PSN/Prime Video, NBA-LPRadio: 98.5 Sports Hub, 92.9 The Game, Sirius XMTD Garden

The Celtics return home from their 4 game road trip to take on the Atlanta Hawks. This is the first of 3 games between these teams this season. They will play 2 more games in Atlanta on February 15 and April 9. They split the series 2-2 last season with each team winning one at home and one on the road. The Celtics are 247-153 overall, all time against the Hawks. They are 133-46 in games played at Boston.

This is the final NBA Emirates Cup group game for both teams. The Hawks are 2-1 and the Celtics are the only unbeaten team in the East in group play at 3-0. To make the quarterfinals for the first time since 2023, the Celtics need to beat Atlanta because head to head matchup would be the tie breaker should the Hawks win. They could make it into the quarterfinals as the wild card team if their point differential ends up the largest. Currently they have the best point differential at +68. Detroit +31, Miami +25, Cleveland +24 and Atlanta +21 could challenge that with a Celtics loss and a blow out win from any of those teams.

The Celtics finally got a win after 4 straight losses and are now 2nd in the East, 2.5 games behind 1st place Philadelphia and they are tied with 3rd place New York and half a game ahead of 4th place Atlanta. They are 11-2 against Eastern Conference opponents. They are 6-1 at home and 6-4 in their last 10 games. They won their last game. The Hawks are 4th in the East, 3 games behind 1st place Philadelphia and half a game behind 2nd place Boston. They are 10-2 against Eastern Conference teams. They are 6-3 on the road and 8-2 in their last 10 games. They have won their last 4 games.

The Hawks made some changes since last season. They brought in Lugentz Dort, Aaron Wiggins, Devin Carter and Ryan Nembhard through trades. They waived Carter and Nembhard before the season. They drafted Kingston Flemings with the 8th pick in the draft, Zuby Ejiofor with the 23rd pick and Henri Veesaar with the 52nd pick in the draft. They lost Zacharie Risacher, Gabe Vincent, Christian Koloko, Tony Bradley, and Keaton Wallace.

After this game at home, the Celtics will be back on the road playing at San Antonio and Dallas. Then it's home against Charlotte before the break for the NBA Cup tournament. Then, they play a game at Brooklyn before they have a 7 game home stand against Washington twice, Utah, Detroit, Portland, Miami and Cleveland. Then it's back on the road for a 6 game road trip through Chicago, Utah, Portland, Phoenix, the LA Lakers and the LA Clippers.

The Hawks are playing in the first game of a 3 game road trip that will take them to New York and Miami after this game. They then host Indiana before the break for the NBA Cup Tournament. Then they go on a 3 game road trip through Portland, Phoenix and Denver before they host Orlando, Oklahoma City, Detroit and Charlotte. Then they are back on the road to play one game at San Antonio before hosting Chicago, Milwaukee and Dallas.

The Celtics have been rotating players at the shooting guard spot. They have started Jordan Walsh, Hugo Gonzalez, Baylor Scheierman and most recently, Sam Hauser. The starting lineup of White, Hauser, George, Tatum and Queta are a plus-54 per 100 possessions and they got off to an 18-9 start en route to an 80 point first half. However, after the game, Joe Mazzulla was iffy about sticking with this lineup. I'm guessing that Hauser starts again, but he could go back to Walsh, Scheierman or Gonzalez. I've listed the Hawks starters from their game notes.

Mitchell Robinson is the only player on the Celtics injury report at this time and he is once again out due to an illness. The Hawks have 5 players on the injury report. Mouhamed Gueye is out after surgery to repair a fractured left foot. Jock Landale is out due to a hip injury. CJ McCollum is out with a left calf strain. Asa Newell is out after undergoing surgery to repair a plantar plate tear in his left great toe. Henri Veesaar is out or the season due to a torn ACL.

Key Matchups Sam Hauser vs Nickeil Alexander-WalkerAlexander-Walker is averaging 21.3 points, 3.5 rebounds, 2.9 assists, and 1.3 steals per game. He is shooting 41.4% from the field and 35.9% from beyond the arc. He is a good 3 point shooter and so the Celtics need to defend him on the perimeter but they also need to keep him out of the paint. Joe may go with defense and start Walsh or Gonzalez here to defend Alexander-Walker.

Jayson Tatum vs Jalen JohnsonJohnson is averaging 19.1 points, 9.1 rebounds, 6.9 assists and 1.1 steals per game. He is shooting 44.7% from the field and 21.1% from beyond the arc. Even though he is not shooting 3's well, the Celtics still need to cover him on the perimeter. They also need to keep him off the boards as he is a good rebounder. Hopefully Tatum can pick up where he left off against the Wizards.

Honorable MentionNeemias Queta vs Onyeka OkongwuOkongwu is averaging 14.5 points, 8.2 rebounds and 2.4 assists per game while shooting 42.9% from the field and 33.3% from beyond the arc. The Celtics need to keep him out of the paint and off the boards, but they can't leave him open on the perimeter because he is capable of hitting 3's. Keys to the GameDefense – Defense is always a key to winning games. The Celtics have an offensive rating of 115.7 (9th) while the Hawks have an offensive rating of 112.7, which is 20th. The Celtics have a defensive rating of 105.8 which is 2nd while the Hawks have a defensive rating of 109.7, which is 6th. The Celtics have been hot and cold on defense this season. Most of the time they can shut down the other team but sometimes they allow them to score at will like allowing the Magic to score 40 in the 4th quarter or the lowly Kings to score 35 in the 3rd quarter. The Celtics need to defend the perimeter because the Hawks are 4th, shooting 43.3 threes a game. They also have to defend the paint as the Hawks are 6th with 51.2 points in the paint per game. The Celtics must make defense a priority in this game and they have to play that tough defense for 48 minutes and not let up.

Rebound – Next to defense, rebounding is the key to winning. When the Celtics put out the extra effort on the boards, it usually carries through to the rest of their game. The Hawks are averaging 45.7 rebounds per game (5th) while the Celtics are averaging 45.2 rebounds per game (10th). The Celtics need to aggressively go after every rebound. They can't afford to give the Hawks extra possessions and 2nd chance points by allowing them to beat them to rebounds. The Hawks are 5th with 16.9 second chance points per game and so the Celtics must keep them off the offensive glass.

Effort and Focus for 48 Minutes– The Celtics have to play with extra effort overall for all 4 quarters. In most of their losses and even in some of their wins, they have allowed their opponents to play with more energy than them for periods of time during the game. They play well for stretches but let up and allow their opponents to surge ahead, especially in the 3rd quarter. The Celtics offensive rating in the 3rd quarter is 96.9 and their defensive rating in the 3rd quarter is 124.8, giving them a net rating of -27.9, which is the 2nd worst in the league. They have to stay focused for all 48 minutes and be ready for their opponents to play harder in the second half and they need to match that effort. They also have to come out with more effort and energy to start the game and not dig themselves into a hole. The Hawks have been playing very well and so the Celtics will need maximum effort for all 48 minutes to beat them.

Move the Ball Carefully – When the Celtics move the ball and find the open man, they are tough to beat. When they hold the ball and try to do too much individually, they become predictable and struggle. The Hawks move the ball well and average 30.3 assists per game while the Celtics are 16th with 26.7 assists per game. The Celtics need to move the ball, but they have to make careful passes because the Hawks are 2nd in the league with 22.6 points off turnovers per game.

X-FactorsHome Game and Cup Game– They call it home court advantage for a reason and the Celtics need to take advantage of playing on their home court. The Celtics need to get motivation from the home crowd, which should be loud and doing everything in their power to support the Celtics and rattle the Hawks. However, it is the first game at home after a long road trip and those games are tough on the home team. The Hawks have the distractions of travel, an unfamiliar arena and a hostile crowd and hopefully that will give the Celtics an advantage. The Hawks have been off since Sunday and so may also have some rust to deal with. Both teams should have extra motivation to win this game in order to advance to the quarterfinals of the NBA Cup.

Officiating – Officiating is always an x-factor in every game. Every crew officiates differently. Some call it tight, others let them play. The Celtics need to adjust to how the refs are calling the game and not allow bad calls or no calls to take away their focus from playing the game the right way. We have seen how much of an x-factor officiating can be in a few games this season. The Celtics have to play so well all game that the officiating, no matter how bad, can't influence the outcome.

#2761. No. 19 Kansas soccer advances to Sweet Sixteen with win over No. 15 UCLA

EAST LANSING, Mich. (KSNT) — The Jayhawks are still dancing.

No. 19 Kansas (14-5-3, 7-2-2 Big 12) defeated No. 15 UCLA (13-5-3, 9-2-1 Big Ten) in the round of 32 on Friday, 2-2 (3-2 PKs).

Marit McLaughlin would open up the scoring just a minute and a half into the game to give the 'Hawks a 1-0 lead. Caroline Castans grabbed the assist.

KU would take a 2-0 lead when Jillian Gregorski knocked one into the bottom left corner of the net at the 47 minute mark. Jordan Rowan and Jocelyn Herrema were each credited with an assist.

Late-game heroics helped the Bruins push this match to extra time, with Kara Croone scoring in the 87th and 88th minutes.

After two scoreless extra time periods, the game would go to a shootout.

The Bruins would net their first attempt, while Livvy Moore's attempt would be saved on the first try for KU.

Sophie Dawe made a big save on UCLA's second attempt, while Castans' look was saved on KU's second attempt.

After another save from Dawe, McLaughlin would net the Jayhawks' first goal of the shootout.

UCLA would net a second goal, followed suit by a good goal for Norah Jacomen.

One last save from Dawe would see Gregorski claim victory with a good goal to send Kansas to the Sweet Sixteen for the fourth time in program history.

The 'Hawks will take on No. 3 Michigan State on Nov. 30 from East Lansing, with kickoff set for 5 p.m.

#2760. Lakers' Austin Reaves available for NBA Cup matchup vs. Spurs, returning after 5-game absence

Austin Reaves will return to the Los Angeles Lakers' lineup for their NBA Cup matchup with the San Antonio Spurs on Friday. He is listed as available on the team's injury report.

Reaves, 28, has been sidelined since suffering a bruised heel in a Nov. 11 win over the Milwaukee Bucks. At the time, the Lakers believed Reaves' injury wasn't serious and didn't require imaging. Head coach JJ Redick said the guard was day-to-day. However, he missed the team's next five games.

During that five-game span, the Lakers went 4-1 while facing lesser teams including the Charlotte Hornets (4-13), Utah Jazz (3-16) and Sacramento Kings (2-16).

In 12 games this season, Reaves is averaging 24.7 points per game, second behind Luka Dončić, who leads the league with an average of 36.6 points. He's also averaging 4.7 assists and 1.2 steals while shooting 41% on 66 3-point attempts.

The Spurs also could get guards De'Aaron Fox and Dylan Harper back for Friday's game. Both are listed as questionable.

The Lakers go into Friday's matchup with the NBA's best record at 15-3, a half-game ahead of the 15-4 Spurs.

In the NBA Cup standings, both teams have a 2-1 record in the Western Conference's Group C. San Antonio would win the group with a win and the Portland Trail Blazers losing to the Golden State Warriors. However, with a +48 point differential, the Spurs could still advance on a tiebreaker.

The Lakers would prevail with a victory and a Warriors loss. But a defeat would eliminate them from the in-season tournament.

#2759. Brock Purdy receives major injury update ahead of 49ers-Seahawks game

The 49ers won't have quarterback Brock Purdy when they take on the Seahawks on Sunday.

Purdy is dealing with an ankle injury suffered during last week's game against the Vikings and Mac Jones will start in his place, coach Kyle Shanahan announced Friday.

San Francisco 49ers quarterback Brock Purdy reacts before an NFL football game against the Minnesota Vikings in Mexico City, Sunday, Nov. 22, 2026. AFP via Getty Images

Purdy sustained the injury in the second quarter of last week's 35-10 victory over the Vikings in Mexico City. He had his ankles taped for the second half before he finished 21-of-26 passing for 255 yards and three touchdowns while helping solidify his case as an MVP candidate.

Shanahan told reporters it was a mid-ankle sprain that didn't heal as quickly as the team would have hoped. Purdy missed practice the entire week, leaving Jones to receive practice reps with the starters.

"Any time it's not a high ankle sprain, you get pretty relieved. But it was more mid than low, which is a little bit worse," Shanahan said. "(I) kinda had a feeling just the way it was by the time Wednesday came around. So didn't heal up as fast as we wanted so we had to shut him down."

Shanahan was asked if Purdy would be available for the following game when the 49ers travel to play the Giants. He said it was too soon to tell.

"But I'm pretty optimistic about it. But I was about this week to start, and then it changes. Hopefully it'll be all right," he said.

The 49ers signed Jones before the 2025 season to a two-year, $8.4 million contract to upgrade their backup quarterback situation after struggling through stints with Brandon Allen and Joshua Dobbs the season prior.

"Mac's been awesome," Shanahan said. "He's had a great week. We went through this a lot last year, so we got a ton of confidence in Mac. Mac's had a hell of a camp, and he's the same guy every day. So it's been a good week with Mac."

Mac Jones #10 of the San Francisco 49ers attempts a pass against the Minnesota Vikings in Mexico City, Sunday, Nov. 22, 2026. Getty Images

The 49ers believe Jones is one of the NFL's best backup quarterbacks after relying on him heavily when Purdy was injured in 2025. Teams called about acquiring Jones at the trade deadline, but the price was reportedly too high. The Vikings were reportedly among those teams and instead opted to swap a third-round draft pick with the Raiders for Kirk Cousins.

"Any time you have a backup quarterback you believe could be one of the better starters in this league, that's a hell of a deal," Shanahan said. "It's always hard and tempting when someone offers a lot of stuff, because you know how much that can help your team. But you're kinda glad you don't have to make that decision if because of how bad it is if your starter gets hurt and you're missing that guy. So really grateful that he's here and that we didn't make that trade."

San Francisco 49ers quarterback Brock Purdy listens to a question after an NFL football game against the Dallas Cowboys in Arlington, Texas, Sunday, Nov. 15, 2026. AP Photo/Julio Cortez

San Francisco went 5-3 in Jones' eight starts last season while Purdy was dealing with a turf toe injury suffered in Week 1. Jones completed 69.6% of his passes, averaging 269 yards per game with 13 touchdown passes and six interceptions.

Jones kept the 49ers afloat while they finished 12-5 and made the playoffs. Sunday's game will mark the 11th Purdy has missed due to injury since the start of the 2024 season.

#2757. Ex-UFC Champion Makes Bold Suggestion for Conor McGregor’s Next Opponent

LAS VEGAS, NEVADA - JULY 11: Conor McGregor of Ireland looks on prior to facing Max Holloway of the United States in their welterweight bout during UFC 329 at T-Mobile Arena on July 11, 2026 in Las Vegas, Nevada. (Photo by Ian Maule/Getty Images) Getty

Former two-division UFC champion Henry Cejudo made a bold suggestion for Conor McGregor's next opponent. Cejudo made a strong case for reigning lightweight champion Justin Gaethje to face McGregor upon his return.

McGregor is currently recovering from his leg injury. The injury occurred during the opening exchange of his return fight against Max Holloway at UFC 329. It was unfortunate for the Irishman, as it marked his first fight in five years.

McGregor is eager to return to the octagon and fight on the final bout remaining on his current UFC contract. He has expressed interest in running it back with Holloway, but the UFC matchmakers will ultimately make the decision.

While McGregor being awarded a lightweight title shot is unlikely, Cejudo revealed how McGregor vs. Gaethje could become a reality.

Henry Cejudo suggested that the UFC book a fight between Conor McGregor and Justin Gaethje. One of the main reasons for his suggestion is his respect for Gaethje, who has always come through for the promotion.

'The Highlight' always puts on exciting fights and Cejudo feels that he should be rewarded with a lucrative fight. Cejudo also pointed to McGregor's contract status and is confident that Gaethje would have a favorable matchup.

With McGregor potentially leaving the promotion, 'Triple C' backed Gaethje in a potential bout to ensure the Irishman does not leave with a win.

"I think there's a big fight potentially – and Kamaru Usman brought this up, so I'm going to credit Kamaru on this one – [Gaethje] and Conor McGregor. No, I don't think for [the lightweight championship]. I think Justin deserves a big payday and I think Conor McGregor is going, it's his last fight, and I do believe that the UFC believes that Justin could beat him. So, I think you do that," Cejudo told MMA Fighting.

"I think [Gaethje could] relinquish the belt and who knows, man, maybe since Dana White loves [Alexander] Volkanovski so much, maybe he should do Arman [Tsarukyan] vs. Alexander Volkanovski [for the vacant lightweight title]."

GettyLAS VEGAS, NEVADA – JULY 11: Conor McGregor of Ireland looks on prior to facing Max Holloway of the United States in their welterweight bout during UFC 329 at T-Mobile Arena on July 11, 2026 in Las Vegas, Nevada. (Photo by Ian Maule/Getty Images)

If the UFC were to book McGregor vs. Gaethje, the bout would most likely be contested at welterweight.

McGregor's latest bout against Holloway, who competes at lightweight, was also contested at welterweight. Fighting Gaethje at 170 pounds ensures that 'The Notorious' does not have to worry about cutting weight or being undersized.

Gaethje could very easily compete at 170 pounds, which could give him a break from having to cut weight as well.

Additionally, contesting the fight at welterweight would also ensure that the lightweight division does not get held up. Gaethje could vacate the title and move up to face McGregor. If he wins and decides to continue fighting, he could return to lightweight for one more fight.

#2754. Broncos, under Bo Nix, are much better at home than on the road

Lost in the ongoing drama in Pittsburgh is that they play the 8-2 Broncos today. Lost in that is that the Broncos, with Bo Nix at quarterback, are a much better team at home than they are on the road.

After playing 23 games in Denver, Nix has a 20-3 record with 43 touchdown passes and averaging 242.6 passing yards per game. Playing 23 games elsewhere, the Broncos are 13-10, with Nix throwing 27 touchdowns and averaging 195.9 passing yards per game.

The Steelers, in contrast, are 4-0 at home this year and 2-4 on the road.

With the forecast for the game similar to the conditions that played out in Buffalo on Thursday night, that could even things out, from a talent standpoint. And it could give the Steelers a chance to rebound from two days of total dysfunction with a season-altering win.

However it goes, the pressure is on Steelers coach Mike McCarthy. The best way to turn the page is to win. A loss (particularly an ugly one) will give even more credence to the things Aaron Rodgers said publicly on Wednesday.

#2748. Blazers Stay Hot, Beat Warriors to Carry Momentum

The Portland Trail Blazers stayed hot on Friday night, defeating the Golden State Warriors 116-94 at Moda Center to improve to 13-6 on the season. That win officially secured West Group C for Portland, which will advance to the knockout stage of the NBA Cup.

This was an interesting test for the Blazers in a game in which not only was Ja Morant out, but so was Damian Lillard. Both starting guards missed this contest, putting more responsibility on the rest of the roster to generate offense.

But in their absence, Deni Avdija was great per usual, producing 26 points, seven rebounds and seven assists. He's been one of the most impactful players in the entire NBA this season. Especially at his size, his ability to facilitate the offense and be such a strong offensive engine for the Blazers is incredibly impressive.

Portland really won this game in the first quarter, holding Golden State to just 11 points in the opening frame while scoring 37 of its own. That set the tone for the remainder of the game and ultimately helped the Blazers build a 36-point advantage before halftime.

Although Golden State did cut the deficit to 10 points in the fourth quarter, the game never really felt that close. This was truly a dominant performance from top to bottom for Portland, which responded with a 14-0 run to put the game out of reach.

Toumani Camara had 20 points and eight rebounds, while Scoot Henderson chipped in 18 points and seven assists without committing a turnover. Henderson's ability to provide that level of production off the bench on a night when both starting guards were sidelined was especially encouraging.

Donovan Clingan added a double-double with 10 points and 12 rebounds to go along with three blocks. Robert Williams III also had 12 points off the bench, giving Portland quality production at the center position throughout the contest.

It was yet another example of the Blazers having seven players finish in double figures, including Jrue Holiday and Micah Potter, proving that the depth of this team and the collective talent on the roster are incredibly impressive.

Especially over the course of an 82-game season, having the ability to win games in dominant fashion without two of your best players is extremely valuable. Portland has built a roster capable of doing exactly that, with a blend of veterans and young talent who can step into larger roles when necessary.

Portland has now defeated three quality teams in a row in the Philadelphia 76ers, San Antonio Spurs and Warriors. The Blazers continue to establish themselves as one of the better teams in the Western Conference and will have an opportunity to further prove that in the knockout stage of the NBA Cup.

Portland will look to extend its winning streak on Sunday when it hosts the New Orleans Pelicans at Moda Center.

#2745. 'I don't see many teams stopping us!' - Morgan Gibbs-White issues bold Premier League warning after inspiring Nottingham Forest to 3-2 win over Chelsea

Morgan Gibbs-White scored a second-half brace to inspire Nottingham Forest to a thrilling 3-2 Premier League victory over Xabi Alonso's Chelsea on Friday night. Speaking after climbing from 17th to 14th, the England international hailed a "perfect night" under the City Ground lights and urged patience with summer appointment Oliver Glasner.

Morgan Gibbs-White struck a second-half double as Nottingham Forest claimed a deserved 3-2 Premier League victory over Xabi Alonso's Chelsea at the City Ground on Friday night. Igor Jesus opened the scoring inside five minutes before Gibbs-White converted a 51st-minute penalty and chipped home a 73rd-minute winner off Xaver Schlager's pass, rendering substitute Emmanuel Emegha's late brace a mere consolation as Forest climbed from 17th to 14th.

"These are what the City [Ground] nights are all about — night-time games under the lights, raining, bad weather but the team showed up," Gibbs-White told Sky Sports. "We showed that grit, we showed that determination, and we got the job done, and I think deservedly as well."

Despite claiming their second successive home triumph, Gibbs-White stressed that supporters must remain patient while the squad adapts to manager Oliver Glasner following his summer arrival. Acknowledging that growing pains will continue in England's top flight, the Forest number 10 urged everyone to back the Austrian coach's methods.

"But I feel we need to take a bit of realization that we have a new manager, we're sticking with him, we're trusting the process, believing in him and trusting what he wants," Gibbs-White said. "Not every week is it going to be pretty or is it going to be nice, but when times like this happen tonight we've got to make the most of it and enjoy it."

All three of Forest's Premier League victories this term have now arrived against heavyweights, adding Chelsea to earlier scalps over Aston Villa and Manchester City. Gibbs-White also singled out Schlager for the incisive pass that created his decisive dinked finish, noting the Austrian midfielder reproduces that quality regularly in training.

"The City Ground is a difficult place to come and especially when you've got us being intense and with that full pressure, and I think we showed that in the first half," he added. "It was an incredible pass and it shows that quality he's got, he's done it in training plenty of times and he's shown that quality."

While enjoying a much-needed weekend boost on Trentside, Gibbs-White challenged his team-mates to match their big-game intensity every week rather than only against top-six opposition.

"We have that hunger and desire, and we need to show it consistently now," he told the BBC. "If we can do that, I don't see many teams stopping us."

Who will win this marquee matchup? Bernd Buchmasser: Let's be clear: the Patriots should win this game even with Tommy DeVito under center. The Chargers have been bad all season and on top of it all will be without Justin Herbert on Sunday; they also had two weeks to get ready for this matchup. Can you fly around, attack the football, and win a game for the team? I'm not particularly optimistic, but I hope I'm wrong. | Patriots 10, Chargers 20

#2739. UNC vs. West Virginia Live Discussion

The Tar Heels are in Charlotte to face off against West Virginia as part of the Dick Vitale Invitational. The Heels are going into this one with just one loss on the season, while the Mountaineers have two losses, both of which were against ranked teams. This will likely be the Heels' toughest test so far, but this is also a bit of a different team from the one that played Georgia. Having Cameron Fens in the lineup has made a difference, and one would argue that Terrence Brown playing point guard in Neo Avdalis' absence has had more of a positive impact on the team than a negative. The thing, though, is his ability to be a facilitator will be tested big in a big way tonight.

The Mountaineers are currently averaging 8 steals per game, which is slightly higher than what Carolina has been doing. Having a team that is that active on defense when you do not have your starting point guard can be tough, so we'll see if Brown is able to take care of the ball. On the flip side, West Virginia is also averaging 9.8 turnovers per game, which funny enough is also somewhat similar to UNC's numbers. This game will ultimately come down to who can take care of the ball better and who will be able to rebound, but of course neither matter if the ball isn't going into the basket. Given how aggressive the Heels have been in the paint — mostly because of the crazy stuff Brown has been able to do — it's hard to see scoring being the big issue tonight.

#2738. Aaron Rodgers got no support from the Thanksgiving broadcasts

The day after former Steelers quarterback Aaron Rodgers went on TV and aired dirty laundry from the Pittsburgh locker room, none of the former quarterbacks (or other former players) who appear on TV supported him.

Across the board, the reaction from the NFL’s analysts and broadcasters was negative.

Former Steelers quarterback and Fox analyst Terry Bradshaw went off. Fox’s Michael Strahan agreed that Rodgers shouldn’t have gone public with his complaints — calling him a “narcissist” and a “rich jerk” and a “rich bad teammate.”

Fox’s Howie Long said that the moment “defines who [Rodgers] is.”

“He’s a guy that I think picked the wrong sport,” Long said. “He probably should have played tennis.”

Fox’s Rob Gronkowski said that, even if a contender loses its starting quarterback, there’s “too much smoke” to justify bringing Rodgers in.

The CBS pregame crew offered similar views. Nate Burleson said Rodgers “didn’t handle this right.” Bill Cowher said he hates to see a Hall of Famer go out this way. Russell Wilson said, “It’s about Aaron Rodgers’s legacy. . . . If I’m his family members, if I’m somebody close to him, I’m his significant other, whatever it may be. ‘Is this how you want to go out?’ After all the success he’s had, is this how you want to go out? . . . This is a disappointment.”

During the Eagles-Cowboys game, Fox’s Tom Brady criticized Rodgers for violating the “sanctity” of the locker room.

The irony, if I’m using the term correctly (I never know), is that Rodgers has previously railed about leaks from the locker room.

“I think it’s chickenshit at its core, and I think it has no place in a winning organization,” Rodgers said about the Jets.

“One thing I really love — and it’s kind of the antithesis of where I was — is there’s not really any leaks in the boat,” Rodgers told reporters during his first year with the Steelers. “Every year, you have difficulties and adversity, both on the field and off the field, and to go through a season like this, and to be able to focus on football and not have a lot of other little bullshit out there has been really nice.”

Rodgers didn’t leak anything. He said it directly, bluntly, unapologetically. He took the team’s focus off of football, at a time when the team was otherwise trying to fight through “difficulties and adversity.”

Again, he isn’t a whistleblower. He’s a troublemaker. He didn’t get his way, so he set the house on fire as he walked out the front door.

As one PFT reader put it, ultimatums and “blackmail” are close cousins. For Rodgers, it wasn’t just “let me call the plays or cut me.” It was, whether he said it this way or not, “let me call the plays or I’ll tell the world all the stuff you don’t want the world to know.”

#2736. Aaron Rodgers releases new statement via Al Michaels, says he went on 'Pat McAfee Show' after Steelers barred him from team facility

Aaron Rodgers made quite a few waves on his way out of the Pittsburgh Steelers organization earlier this week. He had more to say during their first game without him — a game they won 27-5 over the Denver Broncos.

Prime Video broadcaster Al Michaels relayed a text message he got from the former MVP during the game which attempted to rebut some of his critics and explain why he handled things the way he did. Michaels read the text messages early in the fourth quarter of the Black Friday game.

First, Rodgers complained about the idea he has committed some sort of locker room faux pas by airing out dirty laundry involving Steelers head coach Mike McCarthy, whom Rodgers accused of a number of shortcomings during an appearance on "The Pat McAfee Show" on Wednesday:

"A lot of talk about the sanctity of the locker room … The code or sanctity exists to protect the integrity of the locker room, not to provide immunity from accountability for its leadership."

That rebuttal could be aimed at a number of notable figures:

Rodgers' quarterbacking peer Tom Brady said, "The sanctity of an NFL locker room, it's a very sacred place."

Rodgers' former coach Mike Tomlin said it was sad "to see laundry aired out that way in a public forum, those are things that kind of run counterculture to professional football."

Steelers owner Art Rooney II said, "In general, I think, historically, what's in the locker room stays in the locker room. That's the way it's always been. That's the way it should be as far as I'm concerned."

Former Steelers quarterbacks Terry Bradshaw and Russell Wilson both expressed their disapproval.

Aaron Rodgers still has something to say after the NFL's loudest divorce.

As for how things played out with the Steelers, it's known that Rodgers made some sort of ultimatum — reportedly that he wanted to call plays on offense or be released — and the Steelers opted to announce his benching late Tuesday night and then release him Wednesday after he aired his grievances against McCarthy.

Rodgers claimed he got no communication from McCarthy until his benching was announced, and that he only appeared on McAfee's show after the Steelers barred him from their facility:

"They took this public, never heard from the head coach, then they leak at 11:00 p.m. They also barred me from the facility. That's why I went to McAfee."

Odds are this won't be the last time we hear about Rodgers on this.

Meanwhile, his former teammates defeated the Broncos 27-5 on Friday behind a dominant defensive performance.

#2733. In first game without Aaron Rodgers, Steelers throttle Broncos in blowout win

Aaron Rodgers' fiery exit seemed to wake up the Pittsburgh Steelers.

The Steelers had a wild week. They benched Rodgers on a Tuesday after 11 p.m. The next day Rodgers gave an instantly infamous interview on Pat McAfee's ESPN show, blasting Steelers head coach Mike McCarthy, and he was cut shortly after. The entire franchise seemed to be on fire.

Then the Steelers got to the game Friday. It might have been a short-term boost for a team rallying around its new starting quarterback Mason Rudolph, its coach or just each other after a week of controversy, but the Steelers played a tremendous game and blew out the Denver Broncos 27-5.

"I thought our leaders did a tremendous job of bringing the urgency to their [positional player] rooms and it definitely showed up," McCarthy told reporters postgame.

The Broncos were 8-2 coming in and were outmatched from the first minutes of the game. Rudolph played a solid game, completing 9-of-15 passes for 142 yards and a touchdown. The Steelers' defense kept the Broncos out of the end zone. It was an impressive performance.

The Steelers are 7-4 and there's a very good chance they'll head to the playoffs. There are still plenty of questions over McCarthy's ability to lead after Rodgers' diatribe over his habits like skipping walkthrough practices and not watching film (and very few denials of Rodgers' claims), and an on-field issue over whether Rudolph can be good enough to make the Steelers much more than blowout fodder in the postseason.

But for at least one day, the Steelers looked like a brand new team.

DK Metcalf of the Pittsburgh Steelers celebrates with Pat Freiermuth (88) after scoring a touchdown against the Denver Broncos.

The Steelers were supposed to be the team in chaos. They looked far more put together than the Broncos.

Rallying around Rudolph and themselves after a week of controversy, the Steelers had a remarkable first half. A few minutes into the second quarter, after a Broncos punt, the Steelers led 14-0 and had outgained the visiting team 105-8. Even though the Steelers got tackled in the end zone for a safety after that punt, it didn't really slow them down.

"There's a lot going on. Anytime you're going through a situation where time is taken away from winning and your team responds, it is extremely important and gratifying," McCarthy said. "But the reality of this game … this is a player's game."

Late in the first half, Nix continued his awful day. He had already had the ball slip out of his hands for a fumble that was recovered in the end zone by the Steelers, and inside of the two-minute warning he threw a baffling pass. It was hard to tell who Nix was targeting because it went right to cornerback Jalen Ramsey, who returned it 36 yards for a touchdown and a 24-5 lead. There wasn't a Broncos receiver within the vicinity of the pass.

The Broncos were terrible in the first half. They had 58 yards and didn't score a touchdown, while giving up two to the Steelers' defense. But the Steelers had a lot to do with it. They seemed like a team that was excited to put the drama of the week behind and play football.

The Steelers just had to sit on the lead in the second half. The Broncos made that easy by not doing much.

The Broncos couldn't run the ball against a good Steelers front. Nix was really bad. Wil Lutz missed a field goal on the first play of the fourth quarter, and the Amazon broadcast said the Broncos let the third quarter run out and therefore Lutz had to kick to the side of the stadium that was tougher on kickers due to weather. That seemed like an unforced error by Broncos head coach Sean Payton. The Broncos' defense played better in the second half, but Pittsburgh's offense seemed to know that the game was in hand and it just had to not screw things up.

It was a bad performance by the Broncos on a rainy Friday afternoon and perhaps the best one the Steelers have had all season.

There were still reminders of Rodgers and the long week Pittsburgh had. After DK Metcalf scored a touchdown he put up four fingers on each hand, perhaps a shoutout to Rodgers, who wore No. 8. During the broadcast, Amazon announcer Al Michaels said Rodgers texted him and said he was "barred" from the team's facility on Wednesday after his release. It's not like the Rodgers story is going away for the Steelers.

But winning cures everything. And on Friday, the Steelers looked reborn.

A slow Denver drive to end the game will seal the deal for Pittsburgh, who picks up a massive 22-point win after days of off-field drama. Mason Rudolph had a very solid start in a time of genuine turmoil, but it's the Steelers defense who really deserves its flowers, getting two of Pittsburgh's touchdowns and forcing three turnovers from quarterback Bo Nix.

Steelers head coach Mike McCarthy is all smiles as he leaves the field, with a well-deserved win through the noise.

A chant of "F*** you Rodgers" from some Steelers fans is audible on the broadcast. So far, this has been a dominant form of revenge for Pittsburgh after everything that went down with Aaron Rodgers this week.

After a false start penalty and a sack, Denver goes for it on fourth-and-21 — which, down 22 points with less than 10 minutes left, is kind of the only option. But Bo Nix's long pass is easily picked off by rookie cornerback Daylen Everette, as the Steelers collect their third forced turnover of the game.

Jalen Ramsey is flagged for unsportsmanlike conduct on that play, giving the Broncos a 15-yard boost. It's not entirely clear what the flag was for, though Ramsey was nearby as safety Jaquan Brisker appeared make some contact with Pat Bryant after tackling the receiver. The flag may have been intended for Brisker (No. 3) instead of Ramsey (No. 5) — or might be due to an unrelated incident.

Chris Bowell scores the first points of the second half, with a 33-yarder to grow Pittsburgh's lead to 22 points.

That's a beautiful pass from Rudolph, who launches it way downfield to the leaping Wilson for a 37-yard gain.

Wil Lutz starts the fourth quarter by attempting a 53-yard field goal on fourth-and-17, but the kick goes wide right, likely affected by the wind in the open-air stadium. Tough break for Denver, as things continue going wrong for their offense this half.

Courtland Sutton is called for offensive pass interference after a shove on an incomplete pass. And on third-and-long, Jalen Ramsey deflects a pass perfectly to force Denver to fourth down again as the third quarter comes to an end.

This Pittsburgh defense has been really, really fun to watch, even as the score holds in the second half.

A weather update: Per Accuweather, it's currently 40 degrees in Pittsburgh, with windchill bringing the temperature down to a feel of 30 degrees. More importantly, it's raining and windy, making gripping the ball and keeping it in the air a bit harder.

And just like that, a promising Pittsburgh drive abruptly comes to an end, with Rico Dowdle losing the ball and the Broncos recovering.

Denver catches a break, and sets up a chance to try and get some second-half points on the board.

Dowdle converts on third-and-2 with a well-blocked 8-yard gain, before following up with a 27-yard rush to put the Steelers over the midline.

A promising Broncos drive again ends in a punt, after Bo Nix's pass attempt for Pat Bryant on third down is nearly picked off before falling short.

After moving the chains with a 22-yard throw to Jaylen Waddle, Bo Nix finds the space with his feet this time, sprinting on third-and-7 to slide across the first-down line. Good movement from Nix so far this drive.

We have a first down conversion! Denver gets the ball to Jonah Coleman on seocnd-and-1, and the tenacious running back pushes through for a two-yard gain to move the chains. Finally, we will have a drive in this half last longer than three plays.

Steelers have to punt as well. Four straight three-and-outs between the two teams to start this half.

The Broncos and Steelers are trading three-and-outs to start the second half: Denver is forced to punt for the second time this half, and Pittsburgh will get the ball again while hoping to make something of it. Strong start for both defenses…and less so on the offensive end.

The Broncos go three-and-out again to start off the second half, after another pair of incomplete passes from Bo Nix. The Steelers are not letting up on defense, with some more excellent pass coverage to break things up.

Second half is underway! Denver will get the ball first to try and spark a comeback.

#2732. Are these three brothers British skiing's next family dynasty?

Three Britons on a skiing podium, the union jack emblazoned across their race bibs as they lift their skis in celebration.

And then you learn they all have the same surname.

At a slalom race in New Zealand in September, Luca, Freddy and Zak Carrick-Smith completed a sibling clean sweep of the medals, something no three brothers are believed to have ever done before in the history of FIS alpine skiing.

"It was super special," Freddy tells BBC Sport. "The goal is to do the same thing at a World Cup or the Olympics now."

Their success came at the start of a winter season in which the brothers hope to solidify their burgeoning potential as British alpine skiing's next big things.

And could they follow in the grooves of Olympians Graham and Martin Bell, and Alain and Noel Baxter, to become GB's next family skiing dynasty?

Freddy, 19, is on GB's World Cup squad, while his twin Zak and older brother Luca, 21, are on the second-tier Europa Cup team.

And with their parents as their coaches, it is a full family affair.

The boys' mother Emma Carrick-Anderson was a four-time Winter Olympian for Team GB, while father Phil Smith founded Snoworks ski courses, which operate across Europe.

"They know us best, so they know us much better than just a random coach would," says Zak.

"And then as brothers, me and Freddy have been racing our whole lives together, pushing each other, and then Luca ahead pushing us too. I think that's why we've got so far."

Luca adds: "The best part of it probably, for us, is the competition, and I always want to be the best in everything, especially when we grew up.

"Everything you did, you'd always be pushing harder and harder. We all get along really well, and it's just like being with your best friends all the time.

"It's always been pretty incredible, but it has been difficult at times."

Emma is matter-of-fact about that challenge. Asked what was behind the decision to go it alone as a family, she said: "Basically we didn't have a choice. Ski racing is incredibly expensive.

"When the boys were young, it was all just fun. I don't think we really planned that we were going to take this journey to where we are now, knocking on the World Cup door.

"We would never have been able to afford to put them in an academy. So I think we were lucky, I was an ex-ski racer, Phil was a ski racer, we were also ski coaches, so we could do it ourselves. Not all families are quite as lucky."

Each season, they must source about £320,000. Last season, they feared they would run out of money by mid-January.

While some of their funding comes through GB Snowsport and its Alpine Foundation, the rest must be found through sponsorship deals and crowdfunding campaigns.

When BBC Sport speaks to Emma, she is in Colorado with Freddy, where he will race in the giant slalom World Cup this weekend.

There, a two-and-a-half-hour training session on the slopes costs $1,200 (£914). Fortunately, they are able to split that cost with other teams, but with Luca in Finland and Zak in Austria, Emma and Phil - who quit their jobs a number of years ago - effectively run three different programmes.

Emma Carrick-Anderson competed at the 1992, 1994, 1998 and 2002 Winter Olympics for Great Britain [Getty Images]

Coaches, ski techs, fitness instructors, course-setters, drivers, cooks - they have done plenty of jobs on their sons' journeys up the rankings.

Emma describes them as completely different personalities, and yet "all incredibly determined in their own way". But managing the emotions of her three sons as they learn to navigate the ups and downs of professional sport has been the parents' hardest role.

"They're our kids first, so they're humans first before athletes. And I think that's so important because that will help you ride out the tough times," says Emma.

"I was an athlete myself, and it's very difficult sometimes to separate Emma from the ski racer.

"That is something I know a lot of people struggle with, but it's something that we've been really working on with our guys. You know, if you have a bad result, it doesn't matter. You're not a bad person."

Freddy Carrick-Smith celebrates after his second run in Solden [Getty Images]

Thankfully, there hasn't been much in the way of bad results for the Carrick-Smiths so far this season - and Freddy in particular has enjoyed a dream start to the winter.

In October, in the first giant slalom World Cup of the season in Solden, Austria, he finished 12th - the best-ever result by a British man in the discipline at that level.

After the first run, he was in 21st place - and so hard were his celebrations at that point that he snapped a pole.

Zak and Luca were watching his second run from the finish area, while Emma was watching from the wings and Phil from the start gate. Freddy recorded the fifth-fastest time of the day to rise nine places in the standings.

"It gives me a lot of confidence," he said. "I think I knew I had it in me, but after last year's performance in the World Cup [when he failed to qualify for the second run], I wasn't so sure.

"But I was feeling good going into the race, and I thought I could get into the top 30 and have a good second run, so I thought it was possible."

The campaign continues Stateside for Freddy this weekend in Copper Mountain. Only securing his place on the startline after his success in Solden, it is an unexpected yet welcome twist early on in the season - at an added cost of £20,000 that had not been budgeted for.

"We just keep going. You keep going, keep being positive," says Emma. "Things like Freddy's results. it just makes you realise how incredibly far we've come."

And yet they hope there is further to go. Asked about their ultimate goal, twin telepathy comes to the fore.

"We want to be the best in the world," Freddy and Zak say in unison.

#2729. The Steelers vs Broncos final score has never happened in NFL history

Just a couple weeks ago, the Pittsburgh Steelers and Cincinnati Bengals finished with a final score that had never happened before in NFL history. Now 12 days later, the Steelers are at it again. The 27-5 final is the 1102nd different final score in an NFL game.

When we get a new final score in the NFL, it's called Scorigami. Normally when we get a Scorigami it's because a team scores an absolute boatload of points and often something weird happens, usually with two-point conversions. We got a safety and not much else from the Broncos on Friday, resulting in this Scorigami.

I don't need to tell you this was a strange game with all the defensive scores, but the biggest one for our purposes was the Broncos' safety. It was a pedestrian 14-0 when Jaylen Warren was tackled in the end zone, leading to a 14-2 score. The Broncos scored next on a field goal, to make it 5 points on the board.

A 14-5 final has happened, as has 17-5, and 24-5. It wasn't until the Steelers put the 27 on the board that we got a unique score of 27-5.

If Denver had made their field goal, 27-8 was also a Scorigami.

This is the seventh Scorigami of the 2026 NFL season.

September 13: Chicago Bears 59, Carolina Panthers 37

September 13: Minnesota Vikings 39, Green Bay Packers 22

November 1: Cincinnati Bengals 43, Tennessee Titans 36

November 15: San Francisco 49ers 46, Dallas Cowboys 40

November 15: Cincinnati Bengals 38, Pittsburgh Steelers 18

November 22: Dallas Cowboys 51, Tennessee Titans 30

November 27: Pittsburgh Steelers 27, Denver Broncos 5
