#2725. SCORIGAMI: The NFL’s list of unique scores has added more in 2026

There had been 1095 unique final scores in NFL history as of the conclusion of the 2025 season. We are now up to 1102 with the ones added during 2026.

With teams scoring more points in games, the extra point moving out to a 33-yard kick, and teams deciding to go for two more often to gain edges, never-before-seen final scores are still happening despite the NFL playing for more than 100 years. These unique scores are also called "Scorigami".

Many of the Scorigami we are seeing are for 40-plus point games that have high scoring from both teams, but we've also been seeing scores in the 30s that have never happened. It's not just high scores that have never happened, though. A game has never finished in a 2-2 tie, for instance. The NFL didn't allow two-point conversions until 1994, and we've never seen an 8-7 game. A game has never ended in a 9-9 tie, among other scores still out there.

Since 1992, there have been three or more Scoigamis in every season. The average since the start of the 2000 season is seven per year with the high of 14 in 2013 and a low of three in 2022 and 2009.

The 2026 NFL season didn't wait to get the Scorigami train rolling. Two NFC North teams won games on the Sunday of opening weekend with scores that had never happened before. They were the 1096th and 1097th unique scores in NFL history.

Normally when we get a Scorigami, it's because something weird happened in the game. This one wasn't that. The Bears just laid the smack down. Chicago scored 14 points in the first, third, and fourth quarters along with a 17-point third quarter. All of those are regular scores, there were just a lot of them. We had a late extra point blocked, but 59-38 also has never happened.

The Packers missed an extra point in the first quarter, but that didn't trigger a whole lot of unique scores on its own. Everything stayed pretty normal until the fourth quarter when Minnesota scored to take the lead. They converted a 2-point conversion to go up 25-22. Scoring 25 points in an NFL game is weird. Then Minnesota added two more 7-point touchdowns on top of it. A 39-point day is pretty rare. (A 32-22 score has also never happened.)

After a two-point conversion made the score a very normal 21-21, things got weird. A Bengals safety made it 23-21. The Bengals couldn't get a two-point conversion on a later touchdown after the Titans scored, making it 29-28. Then the Titans got a two-point on their next score to make it a seven-point lead at 36-29. The Bengals scored and kicked an extra point and the game sat at 36-36 at the end of regulation. It should be noted that an overtime touchdown would have won the game without an extra point before this season and 42-36 has happened before.

The Cowboys didn't get the extra point after scoring a touchdown, which made it 16-10 in the second quarter. In the fourth quarter, the score was 38-19 when the Cowboys got in the end zone and tried to make it an 11-point game but failed at 38-25. That's when things got weirder. Dallas made it 38-32 then the 49ers scored a long touchdown with a 2-point conversion to get the to the very strange 46 points. 46-32 was a Scorigami, as was 46-39, but the Cowboys went for 2 after their last TD and got it to 46-40. Also a Scorigami.

For the second time on the same day, we saw a Scorigami. And it was the Bengals' second in three weeks. With the Bengals up 31-2, the Steelers scored and went for two to make it 31-11. it's weird because it's still a three-touchdown game and doesn't really help him close the gap but whatever. While 31-11 would have been Scorigami, both teams scored again to make it 38-18. The only 38-point Scorigami winners left now are 38-2 and 38-4.

For the second straight week, the Dallas Cowboys were part of a Scorigami and the Titans get on the board for the second time in 2026. Dallas seems to have the formula — score a ton of points. There are a ton of 51-point games that have not hit in the history of the NFL. Down 51-23, the Titans scored with 31 seconds left. 51-23 and 51-29 have both happened before as has 51-31. The Titans kicking the extra point to make is 51-30 was the difference.

The 27 points is normal, but 5 points is not. A safety and a field goal were all the Broncos could muster on Black Friday. A lot of other scores have happened against 5 points, but not 27. When the Steelers added a fourth-quarter field goal for the only points of the second half, it moved from 24-5 (which has happened once before) to 27-5, which has never happened. Scorigami.

8-7: Someone scores a late touchdown and goes for the win, not the tie

9-9: Two struggling offenses end in a 9-9 tie, or a 6-6 game at end of regulation ending this way

25-18: A few ways to get to 18 then a score to win

32-22: A team playing catch up gets to 22 and a team keeping them away gets to 32

41-41: With 40-40 off the board, lets go one more

42-25: A team up late pushes it to a three-possession game

46-17: It's the lowest number against the very common 17

50-3: It's the lowest number against the very common 3

60-14: It's the lowest number against the very common 14

After seven Scorigamis in 2024, there were four NFL games to hit new finals in 2025, two including the high-scoring Buffalo Bills. here is how each of them got there in chronological order:

This game features two high-powered offenses at the height of their powers, but the Bills started going for two in the third quarter and missed on consecutive attempts. Halfway through the fourth quarter, they sat at 25 points, a rare number. A missed extra point by the Ravens put Baltimore at 40 — not an entirely uncommon number on its own — before the Bills' final push. Buffalo made it 40-38 after two touchdowns and an extra point but failed to convert on the game-tying two-point conversion. They got the ball back and kicked a field goal as time expired to win 41-40.

There are several ties still available on this list, but not as many as you would think. The NFL didn't have sudden death overtime until 1974 when they added a 15-minute frame, so there were a bunch of ties prior to 1974 but then a lot fewer. They modified the rule in 2010 so it wasn't sudden death for a first-drive field goal then shortened the time to 10 minutes in 2017. In 2025, they gave every team a possession. With those recent changes, ties can be more common.

Those rule changes directly impacted this game between the Dallas Cowboys and Green Bay Packers. Dallas didn't win after their first-possession field goal because Green Bay was guaranteed a possession. The Packers only had 4:40 on the clock and drove deep into Dallas territory. After shots to the end zone failed, they settled for a 40-40 tie as time expired.

In the third quarter, the Jaguars were up 23-10 and scored another touchdown. Up 19, they knew the Texans had to score three times to win so they tried to make it a 21-point lead to make it three touchdowns to tie or win. That play failed, and they sat at 29 points with a 19-point lead.

The Texans then scored a touchdown plus a two-point conversion to make it an 11-point game. Another touchdown with a failed two-point conversion made it a 5-point game. Houston scored again to go up 30-29 and tried another two-point conversion to ensure a late field goal wouldn't beat them, but that try failed with 31 seconds on the clock.

Here's where things get nuts. A 30-29 score has happened before. But the Texans returned a fumble for a touchdown to make it 36-29 as time expired. A final of 37-29 and 38-29 has happened, but because there was no time on the clock, there was no extra point attempt, and the final stood at Scorigami.

For Buffalo's second Scorigami of the year, they also topped 40 points. The Buccaneers started the shenanigans in the third quarter when they scored a touchdown to make it 26-21. They went for two to try and make it 28-21 but the conversion failed. Buffalo got to 31 the old-fashioned way, with four touchdowns and PATs plus a field goal, and the score was 31-26. After another touchdown, the Bucs tried to make it a three-point lead with another two-point conversion that failed, sticking them at 32 points. Buffalo scored another touchdown to make it 37-32 and failed the two-point conversion to try and make it a seven-point game. The Bills' final touchdown game them a 43-32 lead and Buffalo pushed it to 44-32 with an extra point to make it a two-touchdown lead with 2:35 left.

#2724. LIVE DISCUSSION: Brooklyn Nets vs. Toronto Raptors, 7:30 PM ET

It wasn't pretty, but when you're the Nets you'll take a win any way you can get one. Brooklyn finally took care of business on Wednesday in Milwaukee, snapping its five-game skid with a 104-97 victory. Two days later, they find themselves in the first half of a back-to-back against a shorthanded Toronto Raptors team.

Toronto is coming off a frustrating 116-111 home loss to those same Bucks on Monday night, and enters Friday with both Kawhi Leonard and RJ Barrett sidelined. That leaves Scottie Barnes — posting career highs in usage, field-goal attempts, and scoring — to carry the offense.

Meanwhile, with Nolan Traore still out, Ben Saraf has been called up to give Jordi Fernandez extra backcourt depth alongside Mikel Brown Jr. and the several other players who bring up the ball. We said it on Wednesday and we'll say it again tonight — these are the ones the Nets have to get. If they want anything to show for this abysmal season, let's mention how they're only two games back from a play-in spot.

#2718. Joe Milton III praises the Eagles, possibly criticizes the Cowboys

Eagles quarterback Joe Milton III returned to Texas on Thursday and threw a significant wrench into the Cowboys’ postseason plans (and, possibly, Philly’s thinly-veiled soft tank).

After the game, Milton reflected on his days in Dallas.

“It was a lot going through my head from when I first got here in Dallas to when I left, all the time I spent doing scout team quarterback, all the times where I was trying to get in the game, when they were up in games, just trying to get more experience,” Milton said, via Nick Harris of the Fort Worth Star-Telegram. “I mean, it was just great coming back here and playing in this stadium in Jerry’s World and seeing the guys that I worked hard with in fall camp and OTAs.”

He then focused on his current team. While possibly adding a little commentary regarding his former team.

“But I mean, it’s about the Eagles,” Milton said. “These guys show up every day. Nobody’s late to anything. Nobody’s complaining about anything. That’s all you can ask for. That’s the most important thing that I love about this team. I just know they’re going to keep fighting.”

The obvious implication is that, in Dallas, some guys were not showing up every day. Some guys were late. Some guys were complaining.

There are surely complaints today, one day after the Cowboys lost their fourth game of the season while scoring 30 or more points.

#2715. Preview: Can Utah snap a 3 game losing streak against the Denver Nuggets?

It goes without saying that this Utah Jazz season has been a colossal disappointment for fans that were expecting a quick turnaround, after years of extraordinary tanking. At 3-16, the Utah Jazz have struggled out the gate, losing close game after close game and have been smacked straight in the mouth with injuries to key players Jaren Jackson Jr, Jusuf Nurkic, and Lauri Markkanen.

While at times tough to watch, there are also glimpses of what this team can become. The Jazz are rarely blownout of the gym and frequently keep games close. But just as often, the youth on this team (namely Keyonte George, Darryn Peterson, and Ace Bailey) find themselves unable to finish off wins or comeback and steal wins. Many games have given fans brief moments of levity and whiffs of a win, only for that hope to be snatched away with Utah's dumb turnovers, rookie mistakes, or just straight-up missed shots. There is also something to be said for the difficulty of Utah's schedule thus far.

This one will be a tough one. The Jazz lack size and will have to contend with former MVP Nikola Jokic. While a somewhat disappointing season relative to other years, the Denver Nuggets are still 10-6 and receiving All-Star caliber seasons from both Jamal Murray and Jokic. They struggle with their depth and have not received the best play from Christian Braun (on a crazy contract); Aaron Gordon as well looks to be a shell of himself, with years of injuries finally catching up to him.

There is hope on the horizon. Word on the street is that Jusuf Nurkic is getting close to suiting up. As of this morning, he was listed as questionable. If Nurkic plays, it could end up being the most important moment in the season for this struggling team.

#2713. Arizona volleyball ends season with 5-set loss on senior night

After advancing to the postseason the last two seasons, the Arizona Wildcats had to find new things to celebrate as this volleyball season wound to an end with the postseason long since out of reach. A win at home on senior night could have been one of those things, but the final match of the season was a microcosm of the season as a whole.

Arizona played streaks of high-level volleyball, but it could not do it consistently enough to win. After two sets of near-perfect play and a 2-0 lead, the Wildcats collapsed. Kansas State completed a 3-2 (25-27, 16-25, 25-19, 25-17, 19-17) reverse sweep. It ended in the most anticlimactic way possible with a center line violation called on Arizona.

"I think we got too lax and thought they were going to roll over, even though we know they had gone to multiple five sets," said Arizona head coach Rita Stubbs. "And then we didn't know how to respond once we took our foot off the gas. Immature."

Both the home and the visiting Wildcats have had disappointing seasons this year after going to the NCAA Tournament last year. K-State came in with 13-12 overall record and 5-12 record in the Big 12. UA was searching for its third conference win and the first against someone other than last-place Texas Tech. Instead, Arizona ends 7-22 overall and 2-16 in league play.

Arizona's outside hitters both had superb outings. Destiny Ndam-Simpson already had a career high 20 kills before the third set was over. She ended with 28 kills on .260 hitting. She had a double-double with 13 digs. She added four assists, an ace, and one total block.

Carlie Cisneros also had a double-double. The junior outside hitter contributed 18 kills and 20 digs. She had an assist, an ace, and three total blocks.

Cisneros was especially critical after Arizona dropped the third and fourth sets then fell behind 6-1 in the fifth. KSU was over a third of the way to a set victory, but the home Wildcats fought back to take a 10-9 lead. Eventually, they had four match points. Two of them were earned off kills by Cisneros, including one on an assist from Ndam-Simpson.

"I think Carlie put the team on her back a little bit and said, 'I'm going for it,'" Stubbs said. "This one ball—and I said it to [the team]—that Harmony [Sample] set, set it on the complete opposite side, and Carlie saw it and went up and blocked instead of backing off. You know, it was like those plays put you in a position to be ready to go and be aggressive."

Between the end of the second set and midway through the fifth, Arizona just couldn't find that aggression often enough. The team had 20 kills in a hard-fought first set. It followed with 18 in a set that it dominated. After that, it was 12 and 10 in sets that K-State controlled. The 12 the UA Wildcats had in the 15-point fifth set equaled or bettered what they had in the two 25-point sets preceding it.

"As attackers, we stopped being aggressive," Stubbs said. "We were going up and swinging at everything in the first set. I mean, we had 20 kills in the first set and 18 in the second. Then we went down to 12, and so clearly not being aggressive. And then the serve and pass game stumped us a little bit. And even when we got a good pass, we were then lax again on defense, whereas we should have been going."

Arizona played hard-scrambling defense for the first two sets. The team seemed to let nothing hit the floor while also out-blocking the opponent.

The home Wildcats had a considerable advantage in almost every stat—kills, hitting percentage, digs, and blocks—when the score reached 15-7 in the second set. By the end of the match, KSU was just a hair behind in kills and hitting percentage. The visiting Wildcats took the advantage in both digs and blocks.

K-State started the match better than Arizona did. KSU was up by five at 12-7, but Arizona went on a 7-2 run to tie it. Cisneros and Ndam-Simpson each had three kills during the run.

Arizona didn't take a lead until 20-19. It reached set point at 24-22 only to have KSU win the next three points to get its own set point at 25-24.

Stubbs called time and her team responded. Three straight kills by Ndam-Simpson had Arizona up 1-0.

It carried over into the second set, which Arizona led wire-to-wire. The biggest lead came at 21-10, and UA cruised to a relatively easy win.

K-State flipped the script in the third set. The purple Wildcats never trailed. Their biggest lead came at 19-11. Arizona used a 5-0 run to get within three points, but KSU just needed to trade runs to extend the match.

The fourth set was effectively over when KSU went on a 7-0 run to take a 10-3 lead. Arizona never won more than two points in a row as the visiting team forced the short set.

It turned out to be longer than expected. Arizona didn't go away when KSU got a 6-1 lead in the fifth. It cut into the advantage a little at a time then used a 5-0 run to take the lead.

From there, it was a back-and-forth affair. Arizona reached match point at 15-14. It had another at 16-15. A third came at 17-16. Kansas State saved them all.

Finally, K-State earned its first match point at 18-17. As Arizona scrambled to keep a ball up, the whistle blew. The home Wildcats were called for a center line violation. A review confirmed it. The match and season were over in the most frustrating way possible.

The Wildcats celebrated middle blocker Journey Tucker, opposite Britt Carlson, and outside hitter Payton Woods before the match. Tucker has been at Arizona for four years and has been a starter for much of that time. Carlson came to Tucson two years ago after stints at Washington State and UCF. Woods joined this season after time at Virginia Tech, Mississippi State, and Sam Houston State.

Tucker's mother moved out of state during her daughter's time in college. She hadn't been to Tucson in a while, but she made senior day.

"It meant a lot for my family to come down here and watch my senior game," Tucker said. "It meant a lot, and I'm super happy. It was my sister's first time seeing me play in a while, so I'm also happy about that. It's just truly a blessing that they took the time out here to come and watch me in person. I always get texts from them about watching me on TV, so for them to come for our last game and see me in person, it means a lot to me."

Tucker got a fifth season when the NCAA implemented age-based eligibility over the summer. Early in the season, she said that she had already spoken to Stubbs about the possibility of coming back for her fifth season as a Wildcat. Those plans no longer seem so firm.

"Still up in the air about my fifth year," Tucker said. "So just really weighing out all my options and just continuing to talk to my coaches and things like that. So you never know what's going to happen. I think luckily I'm still going to be here in the spring to finish my degree."

Being here in the spring would mean the opportunity to practice with the team even if she plans to transfer, but that would depend on the coaching staff.

Three years ago, Sofia Maldonado Diaz practiced with Arizona during the spring despite being in the transfer portal and actively visiting other programs. That is a rarity, but it is something Stubbs has allowed for at least one player who was leaving on good terms with the program. Like Tucker, Maldonado Diaz was with the program for four years and graduated from Arizona.

Tucker plays a premium position. Middle blockers with good size are always valued. She came into the match tied for third in the Big 12 in blocks per set. She's someone who would likely get a good deal of attention—and probably revenue share money that Arizona cannot match—if she opts to go into the portal. She has also completed the four years she thought she was going to get when she first committed to Arizona.

It would be a big blow for Arizona to lose her. The Wildcats lost their other starting middle blocker after last season. She transferred to Miami, which is closer to home. It had Stubbs searching for another one in the portal.

There is a middle blocker in the incoming freshman class, but she has been injured for two years. Arizona also has both Kristen Dear and Marina Vosloo on the roster, assuming that both are committed to returning, but neither has the experience or blocking acumen that Tucker does. That blocking acumen accounted for an outsized portion of Arizona's scoring this season.

#2710. Former NBA lottery pick Rob Dillingham gets punched by Wizards rookie Felix Okpara during G League game

Playing for the G League Westchester Knicks, former NBA first-round pick Rob Dillingham was involved in a scuffle during Friday's matchup with the Capital City Go-Go.

In the fourth quarter of Westchester's 125-104 win, Dillingham was punched by Capital City center Felix Okpara at the top of the 3-point line. Dillingham pushed Okpara on his way down the court, and the center looked at the official for a foul call.

When one wasn't made, Okpara clotheslined Dillingham across the neck and shoulders, knocking him to the floor.

Both teams then gathered in a scrum past the Westchester basket, going off the court as Okpara backpedaled toward the baseline. Coaches, officials and arena security personnel then separated each group of players.

Ermal Kuqo, the Knicks' senior director of minor league operations, and his 6-foot-10, 270-pound frame also got involved, which surely helped provide a significant obstacle to fisticuffs.

Dillingham and Okpara were subsequently both ejected from the game. Dillingham finished with 10 points and 8 assists, while Okpara scored 12 points with 9 rebounds for the Go-Go.

Okpara was the Washington Wizards' second-round pick in the 2026 NBA Draft, selected No. 46 overall out of Tennessee. In three games with the Wizards, he averaged one point and 0.7 rebounds in five minutes of play. In the G League, Okpara is scoring 11.4 points, 8.8 rebounds and 2.4 blocks per game.

Dillingham was the San Antonio Spurs' 2024 first-round pick, selected No. 8 overall out of Kentucky. He was traded to the Minnesota Timberwolves on draft night for a first-round pick swap in 2030 and first-rounder in 2031.

Last season, the T-Wolves dealt him midway through his second season in a trade involving Ayo Dosunmu going to the Chicago Bulls. Before training camp this season, he was moved to the Charlotte Hornets in exchange for Buddy Hield but was subsequently waived. In November, he signed with the New York Knicks' G League team.

In three games with Westchester, Dillingham is averaging 21 points and 6.3 assists per game while shooting 35% on 17 3-point attempts.

#2708. Lobos Make History, Beat Air Force 42-19 to Clinch First-Ever Mountain West Championship Game

ALBUQUERQUE, N.M. (KRQE) –The University of New Mexico football team continues to make history. With a 42-19 victory over Air Force, the Lobos secured their first-ever appearance in the Mountain West Championship Game and earned the opportunity to host the title matchup.

New Mexico's defense set the tone early against the Falcons. Brett Karhu made an early impact with a tackle for loss, while Tavian Combs intercepted Air Force quarterback Evan Medders during the Falcons' second drive, returning it 17 yards.

The turnover helped spark the Lobos' offense. Kiefer Sibley took advantage, breaking through contact and racing 52 yards for a touchdown to tie the game at seven in the first quarter.

Sibley led New Mexico with 115 rushing yards, while Ricklan Holmes added a touchdown late in the opening quarter to help the Lobos take a 14-7 lead. New Mexico continued to find success on the ground and carried a 21-13 advantage into halftime.

The passing game took over in the second half, led by quarterback Luke Moga. He finished with 248 passing yards and three touchdown passes, including two in the second half.

One of those scores came on a connection with Bailen George, who turned on the speed for another Lobos touchdown. George finished with 100 receiving yards and two touchdowns.

New Mexico also held Air Force scoreless in the fourth quarter and finished with a 182-162 advantage in rushing yards.

The 42-19 win gives the Lobos their 10th victory of the season, tying the program record for most wins in a single season.

Moga said the offense still has room to improve despite the big performance.

"I just went out there and let it rip. I know there's definitely some throws I wanted tonight, too. He could've had probably 140 if I would've hit him on an over [route]. So there's definitely things to clean up. We're not even close to perfect, and we've got a chance next week to be as close to perfect as we can be," Moga said.

Head coach Jason Eck praised his team's performance but emphasized the importance of the opportunity ahead.

"They're a good football team, but we've got a really good football team there. And again, we want to go win this game next week and stake our claim to be the best Lobo team ever, which is saying something. We've been playing football over 100 years here," Eck said.

New Mexico will host the Mountain West Championship Game on Friday, December 4, at 7 p.m. The game will air on FOX.

#2707. Bills have a clearer path to the one seed after Broncos loss

Last week, we told you that the Bills only needed to win their remaining AFC games and they would clinch the one seed in the AFC. They could lose both games to the NFC opponents because of tiebreakers.

Now, with the Denver Broncos and Kansas City Chiefs both losing this week, the Bills have a new path to the one seed.

If the Bills win their three AFC East games against the Patriots, Jets, and Dolphins, they would either need to beat the Broncos on Christmas Day or both the Packers AND Bears in mid-December. Denver can top out at 14 wins, but those three AFC East wins gets Buffalo to 13 wins and beating both the Bears and Packers gets Buffalo to 15 wins.

Denver has a pretty easy schedule, with only one game remaining against a team with a winning record. It's the Bills on Christmas in Mile High.

Buffalo Rumblings is SB Nation's home for Bills news, analysis, and conversation. Download the app to connect with fellow Buffalo die-hards and share your take.

Download the SB Nation app on iOS or Android.

In the AFC West, the tiebreaker is the record vs common opponents now that they have the same division record. The Broncos are 4-0 against the Raiders, Chargers, Seahawks, and Cardinals. The Chiefs are 3-1 against the same opponents.

2. Denver Broncos (8-3, better record vs KC common opponents )

3. Jacksonville Jaguars (7-3, win over CIN)

5. Kansas City Chiefs (8-3, worse record vs DEN common opponents)

#2706. The Toronto Raptors can’t lose this NBA Cup game

Some games are nice to win, other games are acceptable to lose, but giving up a victory to the Brooklyn Nets at this point would bring abject humiliation onto the Toronto Raptors fanbase.

Last year, arguably the lowest point of the season was the Raptors' loss to an even worse version of the Brooklyn Nets team in December of 2025. That game saw Michael Porter Jr. give the Raptors a lovely Christmas present of 24 points and 11 boards, outscoring 3 of the Raptors' starters put together. The Nets won that game by 15 points.

That was a dark day. The sun set at 4:00, the TTC was slow, and Scotiabank Arena was a place of despair rather than joy. The Raps need to avoid a repeat of that happening.

TORONTO, CANADA – NOVEMBER 23: Scottie Barnes #4 of the Toronto Raptors dribbles against Michael Porter Jr. #17 of the Brooklyn Nets during the second half at Scotiabank Arena on November 23, 2025 in Toronto, Ontario, Canada. (Photo by Kevin Sousa/Getty Images) | Getty Images

The Nets are playing with both of their best players active, meaning that Toronto will be in for a challenge containing Porter Jr. and Julius Randle both. MPJ is a fantastic jump shooter whose height and accuracy makes him a deadly marksman that Allen Graves and Collin Murray-Boyles will be hard pressed to contain. Inversely, Julius Randle is one of the strongest, most physical players in the league, capable of bullying his way to easy points and rebounds. Scottie Barnes will likely take this defensive matchup, able to meet Randle in the paint more capably than perhaps anyone else in the league, short of Victor Wembanyama.

Graves has seemingly earned more opportunities to prove himself, and will be starting in tonight's matchup. The rookie has shown flashes of serious scoring and defensive ability, and on a night that would normally be a low-pressure matchup for the Raps, giving the rookie more opportunities and a greater defensive responsibility will go a long way to helping his development.

Indeed, not only is tonight marked by a chance to pick up a much-needed win during a rough part of the season, but it's an NBA Cup night, meaning that beyond the special courts, the Raptors have a chance to notch another win during the Group Stage of the former In-Season Tournament.

The NBA broke down what the Raptors would need to advance tonight. While a win tonight, coupled with the Detroit Pistons winning their matchup tonight, and then a Toronto tiebreaker win would clinch it for the Raptors, a few other scenarios involving a Toronto win, or a loss with a small point differential coupled with some good luck in other Cup games, could send the team forward.

Moving onto the next stage of the Cup would give the Raptors a chance to play or something with smaller stakes, rebuild the team's confidence in themselves, and the fans' confidence in the team. Falling out this early with

Avoiding insult being added to injury would be the optimal result of tonight's game. Losing to the Nets last year was a blow to a team that were mere playoff hopefuls. This season, the Raptors have thus far failed to meet expectations, between some poor late-game performances and some serious starter injuries that have driven the Raptors to the point they find themselves at now.

#2704. Lakers blow 20-point lead in NBA Cup loss to Spurs in biggest test of season

SAN ANTONIO — JJ Redick, and the rest of the Lakers, acknowledged the four-game road trip would be a "massive challenge" because of different tests each team would present.

The biggest test of the trip, and the season so far, came Friday night at Frost Bank Center in the form of Spurs, the reigning Western Conference champions.

And for the first 19 minutes, the Lakers looked like a team ready to ace the test. But the problems they had to answer evolved, and they didn't find enough solutions, leading to the Lakers blowing a 20-point lead and suffering a 99-96 loss to the Spurs to close out NBA Cup group play.

Victor Wembanyama, who scored 25 points and grabbed 16 rebounds, celebrates during the Lakers' 99-96 NBA Cup loss to the Spurs on Nov. 27, 2026, in San Antonio. AP

The Lakers fell to 15-4 with the defeat, with the Spurs improving to 16-4 in a battle between the top two teams in the Western Conference.

And though neither Luka Dončić nor Victor Wembanyama had an efficient offensive performance, the Spurs star big man got the bragging rights, at least for one night, in the battle between two of the top contenders for MVP.

Dončić had 31 points, eight assists and six rebounds, but made just 1 of his 13 3-point attempts (14 for 31 from the field) on a night the Lakers struggled with their outside shooting — especially in the second half.

The Lakers shot 28.6% on 3s (6 of 21) in the final two quarters, with the returning Austin Reaves being the only Laker who found a consistent rhythm from beyond the arc.

Reaves (21 points, eight rebounds on 8-of-15 shooting) made all five of his 3s in the second half, including two late in the fourth to keep the game close after struggling with his ball security (seven of Lakers' 20 turnovers).

Wembanyama led the Spurs with 25 points (10-of-25 shooting) and 16 rebounds, while Stephon Castle had team-highs of 26 points and nine assists.

Luka Dončić, who scored 31 points, dribbles up court during the Lakers' 99-96 road loss to the Spurs. NBAE via Getty Images

The Lakers dominated the Spurs early, with nearly flawless execution of their defensive game plan to play off San Antonio's struggling 3-pointer shooters and shrink the floor against Wembanyama.

They held the Spurs to 38.9% shooting in the opening quarter, including 0 for 6 on 3s after the Spurs missed their final six shots (four 3s) of the first.

The Lakers' defensive intensity carried over into the second, with their stout halfcourt defense helping them take a 45-25 lead midway through the quarter before the Spurs cut their deficit to 54-38 going into halftime.

But the script flipped going into the third for the Lakers, who experienced their own offensive struggles. The Lakers scored 14 points on 6-for-24 shooting (2 for 13 on 3s) in the third, with the Spurs tying the game at 68 apiece when Keldon Johnson made a corner 3 with two seconds left in the third.

The Spurs led by as many as eight points in the fourth, but Reaves and Dončić made big shots late to keep the game close before the Spurs closed them out.

#2703. Mike McCarthy just 'focused on winning' after Aaron Rodgers saga

Mike McCarthy helped guide the Pittsburgh Steelers to a resounding 27-5 victory over the Denver Broncos on "Black Friday" after one of the franchise's most tumultuous weeks in recent memory.

The trouble began after the Steelers announced veteran quarterback Aaron Rodgers was being benched in favor of Mason Rudolph at 11:10 p.m. ET on Tuesday, Nov. 24. The following day, Rodgers did not report to the Steelers' facility and instead aired his grievances with McCarthy in an appearance on "The Pat McAfee Show" before Pittsburgh released the 42-year-old quarterback.

The accusations claimed McCarthy had fallen asleep in team meetings and had performed a "1, 2, 3, Packers!" during a halftime huddle breakdown with his team, among other issues. Naturally, it garnered significant attention in the NFL world, with many decrying Rodgers for breaking the sanctity of his team's locker-room bond.

And one of the most notable reactions came from McCarthy's predecessor, Mike Tomlin, who referred to the organization as having a "dumpster fire" on its hands following Rodgers' release.

Following the Steelers' Week 12 win, McCarthy was asked for his reaction to those comments.

"I really don't have one," he replied, stoically, during his postgame news conference. "Let's just be real here. There's only so much time and energy in the day. I think you have to, when you go through things like that, I have to put all my energy into winning."

"I get that this is part of the responsibility and the opinions and comments of others is why I'm in here to answer questions," McCarthy added. "But I'm focused on winning."

McCarthy's approach paid off, as the Steelers were able to improve to 7-4 with their victory over the Broncos. That has put them a half-a-game behind the Cincinnati Bengals in the AFC North race while giving them more breathing room in the wild-card race, as they now are a game-and-a-half ahead of the No. 8 seed New England Patriots.

McCarthy appreciated earning that victory after what he acknowledged was a difficult week for his team.

"It was definitely a hard travel week for us as a football team," he said. "I'm proud of our players, I'm proud of our coaching staff, I'm proud of our support staff and I really appreciate all the other support."

The Steelers were playing at home at Acrisure Stadium in Pittsburgh in Week 12, so it wasn't immediately clear whether McCarthy was using the phrase "hard travel week" metaphorically as a reference to a series of events the team endured amid the Rodgers saga.

Either way, McCarthy came out of the win pleased with his team. He praised Rudolph for being able to effectively guide the team without Rodgers and also praised his veteran-laden defense for its stalwart performance against Bo Nix.

But above all else, McCarthy was grateful for the team's veteran leadership for establishing "urgency" after a meeting they held earlier in the week.

"I think it says a tremendous amount about the team," McCarthy said, referencing the win. "I really probably want to pivot off the leadership council meeting. Everything that was discussed was healthy. A lot of things in there, but what came out of it was urgency. And I thought our leaders did a tremendous job of bringing the urgency to their rooms and it definitely showed up."

"You definitely saw the urgency today," McCarthy later added. "That's what really stood out for me as far as the vibe in the locker room. Our guys do a good job of that. From the get-go, our leaders really had the guys going. And our defense got us started. We played the game that we needed to play. Just very, very impressed and very proud of the way we handled this."

#2702. Torino-Lazio, nuova maglia granata per i 120 anni: debutto sabato

Il club festeggerà il 3 dicembre con un allenamento a porte aperte; la divisa celebrativa è limitata a 1906 esemplari.

Il Torino farà debuttare la maglia celebrativa dei suoi 120 anni sabato 28 novembre alle 18:00, nella partita contro la Lazio al Grande Torino. Secondo quanto riportato da TuttoMercatoWeb, La divisa, realizzata con Joma per la stagione 2026/27, sarà prodotta in 1906 esemplari, ciascuno con un numero seriale sul fronte.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Il club granata celebrerà l’anniversario il 3 dicembre anche con un allenamento a porte aperte allo stadio Olimpico. La società presenta così la nuova divisa: «Nella stagione del 120º anniversario del Club, Torino FC e Joma presentano una maglia speciale per la stagione 2026/27: un'edizione celebrativa che riprende alcuni segni dell'identità granata con uno sguardo rivolto al presente e all’innovazione».

L’esordio in Torino-Lazio aprirà le iniziative legate alla ricorrenza. Nel comunicato, il club spiega: «La maglia celebrativa farà il suo esordio al Grande Torino sabato 28 novembre alle 18:00, in occasione di Torino-Lazio e darà il via alle iniziative che accompagneranno il Club fino a giovedì 3 dicembre e oltre».

Il riferimento al Filadelfia e agli anni Ottanta accompagna il significato attribuito alla divisa: «Dal Filadelfia allo stadio, dagli anni Ottanta a oggi: questa maglia non chiede di guardare indietro, ma di celebrare le nostre radici nel presente. Ci ricorda che la Storia e la tradizione del Toro vivranno per sempre, grazie a chi continua a indossare questo colore e a tenere accesa la fede Granata».

L’edizione è limitata a «soli 1906 pezzi», in richiamo all’anno di fondazione del Torino. Ogni esemplare porta il proprio numero seriale sul fronte. Sul granata acceso, sottili linee bianche riprendono la trama di una maglia da allenamento usata negli anni Ottanta al Filadelfia, dove si preparava la partita del fine settimana.

Il comunicato descrive il passaggio di quella trama dal campo d’allenamento alla nuova divisa, reinterpretata in chiave contemporanea. Sulla maglia compare il toro rampante degli anni Ottanta, indicato dal club come un simbolo capace di unire generazioni diverse di tifosi attraverso i racconti e la passione di nonni e genitori.

Una patch dorata dall’effetto cangiante celebra l’anniversario. Secondo la luce e il punto di vista, la scritta “Edizione limitata”, il logo e il numero 120 lasciano spazio al numero seriale del singolo capo. Lo stemma con il toro rampante è ricamato sul petto, all’altezza del cuore; il 120 ricompare sui bordi delle maniche, realizzati in tessuto elastico a coste. All’interno del colletto è stampata la data di fondazione: 3 dicembre 1906.

#2699. Spurs rally to beat Lakers, advance in NBA Cup

The San Antonio Spurs erased a 20-point deficit to beat the Los Angeles Lakers 99-96 on Friday and punch their ticket to the NBA Cup quarter-finals on the final night of group play.

Victor Wembanyama scored 25 points and grabbed 16 rebounds, adding two assists, four steals and a pair of blocked shots to fuel the Spurs in the battle between the Western Conference's top two teams.

Luka Doncic scored 31 points to lead the Lakers, but Los Angeles were eliminated from the in-season tournament.

San Antonio advanced as a wild card with a win that allowed Portland -- 116-94 winners over the Golden State Warriors --- to clinch the victory in West group C.

Seven of the eight quarter-final Cup berths were still up for grabs going into Friday's games.

The Spurs left it late, but after scoring just 38 points in the first half they out-scored the Lakers 30-14 in the third quarter to turn the tide.

Stephon Castle led San Antonio's scoring with 26 points, coming up with a pair of big three-pointers in the fourth quarter. De'Aaron Fox and Wembanyama drained clutch free throws in the waning seconds to seal the win.

"We're battle tested," Castle said. "We win big games and we stick together when it's tough for us."

In the quarter-finals the Spurs will play the Dallas Mavericks, who beat the Phoenix Suns 113-109 to finish 4-0 in group play.

The Trail Blazers will take on the Memphis Grizzlies, who beat Minnesota 114-109 to eliminate the Timberwolves.

In the East, LeBron James scored 21 points with seven rebounds and six assists as the Philadelphia 76ers cruised to a 111-87 victory over the Indiana Pacers and into the knockout rounds.

Joel Embiid scored 14 points and pulled down 11 rebounds for the Eastern Conference leading 76ers, who finished top of East group B and were joined in the quarter-finals by the Detroit Pistons, Atlanta Hawks and Boston Celtics.

Philadelphia never trailed, leading by as many as 32 to reach the Cup quarter-finals for the first time.

"We're in it," said James, who won the inaugural edition of the tournament with the Los Angeles Lakers in 2023. "Everyone knows about the Cup games, and everyone knows the importance of it and trying to play for something."

In Detroit, Isaiah Joe scored 22 points off the bench to lead seven Pistons players to score in double figures in a 113-98 victory over the Milwaukee Bucks, who led by 16 points early.

Detroit, missing starters Jalen Duren, Duncan Robinson and Cade Cunningham, seized control for good with a 42-point third period and finished top of East Group A.

Atlanta clinched East group C with an 87-86 victory over the Boston Celtics -- who advanced as a wild card.

Jalen Johnson scored 24 points and Nickeil Alexander-Walker added 20 for the Hawks, who rallied from a 17-point first-half deficit on their way to a fifth straight win.

It was knotted at 83-83 with 1:10 remaining, but Alexander-Walker drove for a go-ahead basket, Jalen Johnson blocked a Jayson Tatum attempt in the lane and the Hawks held on.

Absent from the East's quarter-final lineup are the Knicks, who won last year's Cup in what proved a warm-up for their first NBA title in 53 years.

Needing a win and for other results to fall their way, the Knicks erased a 23-point deficit to lead by one in the fourth quarter before falling 108-105 to the Miami Heat.
